*   **Industry:** Residential Mortgage Lending.
*   **Products / Services:** Conventional, FHA, VA, USDA, Jumbo, Renovation, and Construction loans [cite: 16].
*   **Geographic Markets:** Headquartered in San Ramon, CA; licensed in multiple states (approx. 49 states mentioned for competitors, specific count for MasonMac is extensive based on NMLS) [cite: 3, 11].
*   **Estimated Annual Revenue / Volume:**
    *   **2022 Origination Volume:** ~$703.9 million [cite: 8].
    *   **2021 Origination Volume:** ~$2.1 billion [cite: 17].
    *   *Note: The sharp decline from 2021 to 2022 reflects the broader mortgage market contraction due to rising interest rates.*
*   **Employee Count:** Approximately 210 employees [cite: 18]. Comparably lists them in the 201-500 range [cite: 19].
*   **Corporate Structure:** **Employee-Owned Corporation**. This is a central part of their culture and branding, cited as a differentiator in service quality [cite: 14, 15].
*   **Key Leadership:**
    *   **Marilyn Richardson:** CEO & President (Long-time leader, re-founded company in 2005) [cite: 15].
    *   **Chuck Iverson:** President [cite: 20].
    *   **Herb Tasker:** Chairman (Industry veteran, former MBA Chairman) [cite: 15].
*   **Competitive Positioning:** Positions itself as a "legacy" brand (100+ years of history) with modern tech ("MasonMac"). They compete on service reliability for loan officers rather than lowest consumer rates, targeting the "purchase" market via referral partners [cite: 6].
*   **Strategic Challenges:**
    *   **Litigation:** In July 2023, **Axia Home Loans sued Mason-McDuffie**, alleging the company "poached" 28 employees and misappropriated trade secrets. The suit claims MasonMac executives (including former Axia staff) coordinated a raid on Axia's branches in FL, MN, OR, and WA [cite: 8].
    *   **Market Contraction:** The drop in volume from $2.1B (2021) to ~$700M (2022) indicates significant pressure from the interest rate environment, necessitating the aggressive (and litigious) recruitment strategy to regain volume.
*   **Recent News:**
    *   **Growth amidst downturn:** Iverson publicly stated in 2023 that MasonMac was "growing" despite the hard year, attributing this to attracting sales teams from other struggling lenders [cite: 7].

### 6. Prior Companies (High-Level Only)

*   **Evergreen Home Loans (2020–2021):** EVP of Production. Hired to drive growth; emphasized digital closing tech.
*   **Sierra Pacific Mortgage (1997–2020):** EVP / Production Leader. Pivotal in scaling firm from regional to national; grew retail channel significantly.
*   **Commerce Security Bank (Prior to 1997):** Regional Management. Established wholesale lending offices.

### 7. Public Thought Leadership

*   **Podcasts:**
    *   *California MBA "Connect" Podcast (Aug 2021, Apr 2023):* Discussed post-2008 lessons, liquidity management, and the importance of advocacy [cite: 5].
    *   *HousingWire "Ten Minute Talks" (Apr 2023):* Discussed MasonMac's growth strategy and his philosophy on team building [cite: 2, 21].
*   **Conference Appearances:**
    *   **Mortgage Innovators Conference:** Served as Inaugural Chair (2019) and continues to actively support the event, focusing on the intersection of tech and human advice [cite: 7, 9].
    *   **IMN Mortgage AI:** Speaker on mortgage banking leadership [cite: 22].
*   **Media Mentions:** Featured in *HousingWire* and *National Mortgage Professional* regarding his executive appointments and views on borrower experience [cite: 9, 14].

### 8. Non-Business Activities

*   **Board Memberships:**
    *   **California Mortgage Bankers Association (CMBA):** Long-time Board of Directors member; active in legislative advocacy and event organization [cite: 5, 13].
*   **Personal Interests:**
    *   **Mentorship:** Cites Dale Carnegie training as a major influence; passionate about "coaching" and "team building" [cite: 2].
    *   **Community:** Emphasizes building community within his teams, a philosophy he attributes to his early career training [cite: 2].

*   **CEOs scaling from Regional to National:** Iverson has done this specifically with Sierra Pacific and is currently attempting it with MasonMac.

*   **Operational Scaling:** Can advise on the structural changes needed to move from a volume of $500M to $2B+, specifically regarding the balance of retail vs. wholesale channels.
*   **Crisis Management:** Can share "battle-tested" liquidity preservation strategies from the 2008 crisis, such as "daily huddles" and product shedding [cite: 5].
*   **Recruitment Strategy:** Offers a blueprint for attracting high-producing sales teams during market downturns (contrarian growth).

*   **Risk Tolerance:** Demonstrates a high tolerance for aggressive expansion during downturns (e.g., poaching lawsuits), viewing market chaos as a "land grab" opportunity for talent.

### 13. Strategic Context & Competitive Position

*   **Industry Maturity:** Mature / Distressed. The mortgage industry is currently contracting due to interest rate hikes, forcing consolidation.
*   **Market Pressures:** Extreme volume compression (down ~60-70% from 2021 peaks), margin compression, and fierce competition for the few performing loan officers.
*   **Company Strategic Inflection Point:** Mason-McDuffie is pivoting from a "legacy" firm to a "modern" lender ("MasonMac"). They are aggressively expanding via recruitment (lift-outs) to gain market share while others exit.
*   **Competitive Threats:** Large non-bank lenders (Rocket, UWM) dominating via price; mid-sized lenders (like Axia) engaging in legal warfare to retain talent.

*   **Innovation vs. Optimization Bias:** **Balanced.** Advocates for innovation (Chair of Innovators Conference) but emphasizes that tech must "optimize" the human relationship, not replace it. He is skeptical of "disruptors" who don't understand the complexity of the mortgage process [cite: 5].
*   **Cultural Leadership:** **Tribe-Building.** Uses language like "family," "community," and "MasonMac" to create an in-group identity that aids retention [cite: 2, 14].
*   **Crisis Handling:** **Direct & Communicative.** Believes in over-communication and rapid decision-making (e.g., killing products immediately) to preserve liquidity [cite: 5].

### 15. Unique Credibility Signals

*   **Litigated Growth:** His involvement in high-stakes recruitment wars (Axia v. MasonMac) signals a leader who plays "hardball" to acquire revenue-generating assets [cite: 8].

# Deep Research Report: Doug Bergman

### 1. Executive Summary

*   **Identity:** President and CEO of United Cerebral Palsy (UCP) of Sacramento and Northern California since 2006; Founder and Board Member of Mission Resource Solutions, Inc. (MRSI).

*   **Primary Industries:** Non-Profit Human Services, Healthcare/Disability Services, Software as a Service (SaaS) for Caregivers.

*   **Regulatory Compliance:** Deep expertise in navigating California’s developmental disability entitlement system (Lanterman Act) and federal mandates like the 21st Century Cures Act (EVV requirements) [cite: 8, 10].

### 5. Current Company Overview – UCP of Sacramento & N. California / MRSI

#### **UCP of Sacramento & Northern California (Parent Non-Profit)**
*   **Founded:** 1955 (Affiliate of national UCP) [cite: 7].

### 9. Collaboration & Matchmaking Signals

### 11. What Other Leaders Can Learn From This Person

*   **The "Hybrid" Model:** How to successfully blend a "heart-led" mission with "head-led" corporate financial discipline. Bergman demonstrates that non-profits can be profitable and technologically advanced.
*   **Problem-First Innovation:** The creation of MRSI/Respitrack didn't start as a business idea; it started as an operational fix for internal paper logs. Leaders can learn to monetize internal solutions.
